L2609: ## **18.6.5.3 Criteria for Evaluation (Endpoints)**
L2610: ## **Pharmacokinetics**
L2611: The following pharmacokinetic parameters were calculated for both artesunate and DHA: area under the plasma concentration curve (AUC) from time 0 to the time of the last quantifiable concentration (AUC_{0-last}), AUC extrapolated to infinity (AUC_{0-\infty}), terminal half-life (T_{1/2}), plasma clearance (CL), and volume of distribution (Vd). The predicted concentration at the end of infusion (C_{033}) was calculated for artesunate only, and the C_{max} and T_{max} were calculated for DHA only.
L2612: ## Safety
L2613: Safety was assessed through AEs, cardiovascular assessments primarily ECG, vital signs, clinical laboratory assessments (blood chemistry, hematology and urinalysis), and physical exams.
L2614: **Cardiac** – See QT-IRT review (Section 10.4.9
L2615: ## 18.6.5.4 Key Inclusion Criteria
L2616: Healthy adult males and non-pregnant, non-lactating females 18 to 55 years of age (inclusive).
L2617: ## 18.6.5.5 Statistical Analysis Plan
L2618: Safety was analyzed using the safety population which consisted of all randomized subjects who received study drug and had any post-baseline data. Pharmacokinetic analyses were done using both the intention-to-treat (ITT) population. The ITT population was all randomized subjects who received study drug. The evaluable population was subject who completed the study through Day 2. All subjects met this requirement. The ECG data set consisted of all ECGs from all subjects who were dosed. All available post-treatment QTc data with a corresponding baseline value on Day -1 was used.
L2619: Summary statistics (n, mean, median, minimum, and maximum) for demographics were to be presented including age, sex, race, weight, height, tobacco history, and BMI for each treatment group. Individual subject data listings were to be generated for medical history and physical examination.
L2620: ## Safety Analysis
L2621: AEs were coded according to the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Affairs (MedDRA) version (8.0). Treatment emergent AEs are summarized by System Organ Class (SOC) and MedDRA preferred term for each treatment group. Summaries are presented for AEs by seriousness, severity, and relation to study drug. A data listing of all information relating to AEs is provided. Vital sign data (pulse rate, BP, temperature, respiratory rate, height, and weight) is summarized, and the number of subjects with substantial increases or decreases in BP (20 mmHg) and HR (&gt;15 bpm) is tabulated.
L2622: Laboratory data were summarized and presented with descriptive statistics (n, mean, median, standard deviation, minimum, and maximum) were to be calculated for each laboratory parameter at baseline and each scheduled time point, as well as for change from baseline. Time to greatest change and greatest change from baseline regardless of time was also summarized
L2623: See Section 9 – Clinical Pharmacology for SAP for pharmacokinetics.
L2624: See QT-IRT for Review of Cardiac Data Analysis

L2633: ## **18.6.5.6.2 Treatment Emergent Adverse Events**
L2634: There were no SAEs observed in this study.
L2635: The most common AEs, occurring in \ge 5\% of artesunate subjects, were dysgeusia, dizziness, headache, nausea, vomiting, fatigue, myalgia, and anemia. Of these, only dysgeusia was dose-dependent and was only observed in 5 of 6 (83%) patients in each of the 4.0 and 8.0 mg/kg dose groups and one of the placebo control subjects. The dysgeusia was described consistently as a transient metal, bitter, or garlic taste that started within a few minutes of the IV artesunate infection and lasted 10 to 25 minutes. There were no subjects in the lower artesunate dose groups that experienced this AE. Three subjects receiving artesunate were reported to have mild anemia. All the TEAEs were considered mild or moderate and resolved without treatment.
L2636: **Reviewer comments:** In the CSR the Applicant asserts that the three cases of anemia may have been in part due to the volume of blood collected for pharmacokinetic and safety studies. Plot of hematocrit and hemoglobin over time does show a decrease following study Day 1 for both the placebo and artesunate groups. See results of Laboratory Analysis, below.
L2637: One subject, a 38-year old male who received artesunate at 8.0 mg/kg experienced urticaria that started on the day of dosing which was considered moderate in severity and resolved in one day after receiving Benadryl.
L2638: TEAEs by pooled artesunate are listed in the table below.

L2664: ## 18.6.5.6.4 Vital Signs
L2665: Transient increases in systolic blood pressure of more than 29 mmHg was observed in 2 subjects in the control group as well as two subjects in each of the following artesunate dose groups: 0.5 mg/kg, 1.0 mg/kg, and 2.0 mg/kg and one subject in the 8.0 mg/kg group. One subject in the placebo group and 4 subjects receiving artesunate (1 in the 0.5 mg/kg group and 3 in the 1.0 mg/kg group had systolic blood pressure increases &gt; 140 mmHg in the 8 hour post infusion period. In addition, 2 subjects receiving 1.0 mg/kg and 1 subject receiving 4.0 mg/kg had diastolic blood pressure changes greater than 90 mmHg. All changes returned to &lt;140 (systolic) and &lt; 90 (diastolic) within 24 hours post dose.
L2666: Changes in HR \ge 15 bpm during the first 8 hours after infusion occurred in 6 artesunate recipients, and 2 placebo controls. No episodes of tachycardia (HR &gt;101 bpm) were noted.
L2667: ## 18.6.6 WRAIR Study 1142
L2668: **Title:** A Phase 1 Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Randomized, Multiple Dose Escalation Study to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerance, and Pharmacokinetics/Pharmacodynamics of a New GMP Formulation of Intravenous Artesunate in Healthy Subjects
L2669: ## 18.6.6.1 Objectives
L2670: ## **Primary:**
L2671: - To determine the safety of a single dose administration of artesunate in doses that bracket the anticipated compassionate use dose of 2.4 mg/kg of artesunate by measuring adverse events (AEs) and cardiovascular responses [heart rate (HR), blood pressure (BP), and electrocardiogram parameters (ECG)].
L2672: ## **Secondary**
L2673: - To determine the safety and tolerability of the compassionate use anticipated dose of 2.4 mg/kg of artesunate given intravenously (IV).
- To assess the pharmacokinetics of artesunate and its major metabolite dihydroartemisinin (DHA) in healthy volunteers.
L2674: ## 18.6.6.2 Study Design
L2675: This study was a Phase 1a, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, inpatient, ascending single IV dose study to determine the safety, tolerability, and pharmacokinetics of Intravenous Artesunate in healthy male and non-pregnant, non-lactating female subjects aged 18 to 55 years old. Five cohorts of 8 volunteers per cohort were studied. Within each cohort, 6 subjects
L2676: received a single dose of Intravenous Artesunate infused over a period of 2 minutes at one of 5 specified dose levels, 0.5 mg/kg, 1.0 mg/kg, 2.0 mg/kg, 4.0 mg/kg, and 8.0 mg/kg, and 2 subjects in each cohort received a similar infusion of placebo (mannitol in phosphate buffer). Subjects underwent an outpatient screening visit for medical history, vital signs, physical exam (with a standardized neurologic assessment), hematology parameters including complete blood cell (CBC) count with platelets and differentials, coagulation indices, blood chemistries, urinalysis, urine drug screen, and, if female, a serum pregnancy test. Within 21 days of initial screening visit, eligible subjects were scheduled for a 6-hour outpatient visit in which pre-dose ECGs and vital signs were obtained over the same period of time to compare with those obtained during investigational product dosing on Day 1.
L2677: On Day 0, subjects were admitted to the CPU to begin the inpatient phase of the study and underwent a brief physical exam. On Day 1, pre-dose vital signs and ECG were performed. Subjects then received investigational product. Subjects were closely monitored by evaluating vital signs (pre-dose, approximately 5 min, 20 min, 40 min, 1 h, 2 h, 4 h, 6 h, 8 h, and 24 h after completion of infusion), ECG (pre-dose, and approximately 5 min, 20 min, 40 min, 1 h, 2 h, 4 h, 6 h, and 24 h after completion of infusion, and assessment of spontaneously reported AEs over the next 24 hours. Blood was drawn for pharmacokinetic pre-dose, approximately 5 min, 20 min, 40 min, 1 h, 2 h, 4 h, 6 h, and 24 h post-dose, and when directed by AEs. Blood for hematology, chemistry, and coagulation marker analysis was collected 6 h and 24 h post dose. Urinalysis was also performed 6 h and 24 h post dose. On Day 2, subjects were discharged. Outpatient follow-up included Day 4 vital signs, ECG, hematology, blood chemistry, and urinalysis, Day 8 hematology, vital signs, and ECG, and Day 15 hematology, vital signs, ECG, urine pregnancy test, and physical exam.

L2681: ## **18.6.6.3 Key Inclusion Criteria**
L2682: Healthy adult males and non-pregnant, non-lactating females 18 to 55 years of age (inclusive).
L2683: ## **18.6.6.4 Endpoints**
L2684: ## **Pharmacokinetics:**
L2685: The following pharmacokinetic parameters were calculated for both artesunate and DHA: area under the plasma concentration curve (AUC) from time 0 to the time of the last quantifiable concentration (AUC0-last), AUC extrapolated to infinity (AUC0-\infty), terminal half-life (T1/2), plasma clearance (CL), and volume of distribution (Vd). The predicted concentration at the end of infusion (C0.033) was calculated for artesunate only, and the Cmax and Tmax were calculated for DHA only.
L2686: ## **Safety:**
L2687: Safety was assessed through AEs, cardiovascular assessments primarily ECG, vital signs, clinical laboratory assessments (blood chemistry, hematology and urinalysis), and physical exams.
L2688: ## **18.6.6.5 Statistical Analysis Plan**
L2689: Safety was analyzed using the safety population which consisted of all randomized subjects who received study drug and had any post-baseline data. Pharmacokinetic analyses were done using both the intention-to-treat (ITT) population. The ITT population was all randomized subjects who received study drug. The evaluable population was subject who completed the study through Day 2. All subjects met this requirement. The ECG data set consisted of all ECGs from all subjects who were dosed. All available post-treatment QTc data with a corresponding baseline value on Day -1 was used. Summary statistics (n, mean, median, minimum, and maximum) for demographics were to be presented including age, sex, race, weight, height, tobacco history, and BMI for each treatment group. Individual subject data listings were to be generated for medical history and physical examination.
L2690: ## **Safety Analysis**
L2691: TEAEs were coded according to the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Affairs (MedDRA) version (8.0). TEAEs are summarized by System Organ Class (SOC) and MedDRA preferred term for each treatment group. Summaries were presented by seriousness, severity, and relation to study drug. Vital sign data (pulse rate, BP, temperature, respiratory rate, height, and weight) were summarized, and the number of subjects with substantial increases or decreases in BP (20 mmHg) and HR (&gt;15 bpm) was tabulated.
L2692: Laboratory data were summarized and presented with descriptive statistics (n, mean, median, standard deviation, minimum, and maximum) were to be calculated for each laboratory parameter at baseline and each scheduled time point, as well as for change from baseline. Time to greatest change and greatest change from baseline regardless of time was summarized.
L2693: See Section 6 – Clinical Pharmacology for discussion of analysis of PK.
L2694: See QT-IRT report for discussion of ECG and QT analysis.
L2695: ## **18.6.6.6 Results – Safety**
L2696: ## **18.6.6.6.1 Demographics at Baseline**
L2697: Demographics among the artesunate and groups appeared evenly distributed. Of note, 24/26 (92.3%) of subjects were male.
L2698: ## **18.6.6.6.2 Treatment Emergent Adverse Events**
L2699: There were no SAEs observed in this study.
L2700: The most common TEAE observed in recipients of artesunate were dysgeusia (16 subjects or 80%), headache (6 subjects or 30%), anemia (5 subjects or 25%), dizziness (3 subjects or 15%).
L2701: All of these occurred more commonly in subjects administered artesunate compared with those receiving placebo (16 to 1 for dysgeusia and 3 to 0 for dizziness). Dysgeusia appears to be dose dependent, with 4 of 6 patients (67%) in the 2 mg/kg group and 5 of 6 patients (87.7%) of subjects in both the 4 mg/kg and 8 mg/kg groups experiencing this TEAE.

L2708: **Reviewer comments:** TEAEs were analyzed by dose regimen and no dose-response relationship was observed; however, it was difficult to make meaningful comparisons due to the small numbers of subjects.
L2709: No definition of neutropenia was found in the study protocol. One subject who received Artesunate for Injection at 8.0 mg/kg/dose was coded as having neutropenia classified as mild on Day 3 (1.3 \times 10^6/L) with a further drop in on day 4 to 0.9 \times 10^6/L. Analysis of the full dataset showed an additional 3 subjects with neutrophil counts between 1.0 and 1.5 \times 10^6/L: two subjects received 8.0 mg/kg/dose and one subject received placebo.

L2729: **Reviewer comments:** Mild, transient increases in ALT and AST were observed after dosing with Artesunate for Injection with peaks between days 4 and 7. Two patients experienced elevations rated as mild TEAEs: Subject 4007 (4 mg/kg/dose) had elevation of ALT and AST on day 7 of 292 U/L and 112 U/L respectively; and Subject 5030 (8 mg/kg/dose) had elevation of ALT and AST on day 7 of 166 U/L and 70 U/L respectively. Elevations declined by day 15 for both patients.
L2730: ## Vital Signs
L2731: Transient increases in systolic blood pressure &gt; 20 mmHg and heart rate were noted for each of the dose groups as well as placebo recipients. No dose related changes in vital signs were noted.

L2744: ## **18.6.8.2 Study Results – Safety**
L2745: ## **18.6.8.2.1 Deaths and Serious Adverse Events**
L2746: There were two patients (# [b] (6) and [b] (6)) who died during the study. Both had cerebral malaria. Patient # [b] (6) died on day 2 after experienced seizures and coma; patient [b] (6) died on day 1 following a grand mal seizure progressing to coma. Both patients received the 5 dose regimen. See further details in Section 10.4.1.
L2747: A total of 23 patients experienced 31 SAEs, 13 in Group A and 18 in group B. The most common SAE was anemia in 5 patients and seizures (Preferred Term grand mal convulsions or convulsions) in 4 subjects.

L2754: ## 18.6.8.2 Treatment Emergent Adverse Events
L2755: During the trial, 133 subjects (68.6%) experienced at least one adverse event, 68 (68%) in the 3-dose group, and 65 (69.1%) in the 5-dose group.
L2756: The most common TEAEs by preferred term were anemia in a total of 30 of 194 patients (15.4%), cough in 28 (14.4%), abdominal pain in 20 (10.3%), and pyrexia in 18 (9.3%).
L2757: ## Neurological outcomes
L2758: While EDCTP-MMV included neurological outcomes as a secondary endpoint, limited information was found regarding neurological follow up in the clinical study report. Of the four patients with SAEs of SOC nervous system disorders and Preferred Term “convulsions” or “grand mal convulsions”, two died (Subjects # (b) (6) and (b) (6)). In the remaining two patients,
L2759: the SAEs were considered resolved: Subject # [REDACTED] (b) (6) on day 2, and Subject # [REDACTED] (b) (6) by the outpatient follow up visit on day 28.
L2760: ## **18.6.8.2.3 Laboratory Evaluation**
L2761: The CSR reported “clinically significant” changes for the following laboratory parameters: hematocrit, hemoglobin, MCV, platelets, RBCs and WBCs.

L2887: ## DIVISION OF ANTI-INFECTIVE PRODUCTS
## PHARMACOLOGY/TOXICOLOGY REVIEW AND MEMO TO FILE
L2888: DATE: 2/11/19
L2889: Application number: 64,769
Supporting document/s: 138
Sponsor's letter date: 12/10/2018
CDER stamp date: 12/10/2018
Product: IV Artesunate
Indication: Initial treatment of severe [REDACTED] (b) (4) malaria in adults and children
Sponsor: Amivas, LLC (co-development partner for US Army Medical Material Development Activity, USAMMDA)
Review Division: Division of Anti-Infective Products
Reviewer: Kelly Brant, MPH, Ph.D., DABT
Supervisor/Team Leader: Terry J. Miller, Ph.D.
Division Director: Sumathi Nambiar, MD
Project Manager: Gregory DiBernardo
L2890: ## 1. BACKGROUND
L2891: The Sponsor is intending to apply for a marketing authorization of IV artesunate under section 505(b)(2) of the 21 U.S.C. 9 FD&amp;C Act and has received a Breakthrough Therapy Designation (9/11/18) and Orphan Drug Designation. Artesunate has marketing authorization in 32 countries (28 African and 4 Asian) for treatment of severe malaria in adults and children. Artesunate is also available in several European countries and Canada through the Named Patient Programmes. The proposed dosing regimen for IV Artesunate is 2.4 mg/kg at 0, 12, 24, [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L2892: The parenteral AS manufactured by Guilin Pharmaceutical Company used to treat malaria outside the US lacks approval by the FDA. To meet the need for a safe and effective antimalarial for the treatment of severe [REDACTED] (b) (4) malaria in patients in the US, a formulation of IV artesunate has been manufactured by the US Army under the current IND (64, 769). The Surgeon General, Department of the Army requested a Type C meeting to obtain Agency concurrence on the non-clinical studies to be included in the NDA (a separate pre-NDA Clinical and CMC meeting have also been requested).
L2893: The nonclinical studies were conducted with either Guilin or the US Army formulations of artesunate (manufactured by [REDACTED] (b) (4)) and the Sponsor submitted bioequivalence information (study reports and/or published literature) of the two IV formulations carried out in rats and dogs. The Sponsor indicated that the [REDACTED] (b) (4) API used in the nonclinical and clinical studies under IND 64, 769 will no longer be manufactured by [REDACTED] (b) (4) as they will only manufacture the semi-synthetic produced API in the future with a DMF registered with the Agency. A Letter of Authorization has been granted for
L2894: this DMF. The change from (b) (4) to semi-synthetic API was subject of a separate Type A meeting request (IND 64, 769, SN 0128). Comparative data (b) (4) will be presented in the pre-NDA CMC briefing document when the data are available. The Sponsor intends to use the semi-synthetic (b) (4) source of API for registration batch manufacturing and has proposed (b) (4)

L2907: ## 2.3 Planned Non-Clinical Studies for IV artesunate*:
L2908: (b) (4)
L2909: ## 3. NONCLINICAL SUMMARY OF STUDIES REVIEWED AND PROPOSED TOXICOLOGY PROGRAM
L2910: Safety pharmacology studies in telemetered beagle dogs administered IV artesunate at doses up to 50 mg/kg revealed no cardiovascular or respiratory changes. An additional non-GLP study in monkey given IV artesunate did not include any neurobehavioral assessments of the animals. The Sponsor has proposed to fill this gap in safety pharmacology by including a functional observational battery (FOB) in their 4-week repeated dose toxicology study in rat.
L2911: Changes in hematological parameters were observed following repeat dosing with IV artesunate. A significant reduction in RBC, HCT, and RETIC at \ge 20 mg/kg IV artesunate was observed in female dogs after 14 days. Male dogs also showed evidence of reduced RBC, HCT, and RETIC following administration of IV artesunate, but these findings only reached statistical significance for RETIC at doses \ge 35 mg/kg. All noted hematological changes had resolved by end of the 14-day recovery period. A second GLP repeat-dose study was not submitted. In a non-GLP 7-day repeat-dose toxicity of IV artesunate in rhesus monkey (up to 128 mg/kg), 3 of the 4 animals treated with 128 mg/kg artesunate died prematurely. Surviving animals displayed leukopenia, neutropenia, red blood cell loss, and reticulocytopenia that resolved by the end of a 14-day recovery period. Also observed were increases in AP and BUN in animals administered \ge 16 mg/kg artesunate, however histologic correlates cannot be determined as histopathology findings were not provided in the study report. The Sponsor has proposed a 4-week repeat dose study with 28-day recovery period in rat and dog using the semi-synthetic API to support marketing of IV artesunate.
L2912: Artesunate was negative in the Ames, mammalian chromosome aberration assay (CHO), and in vivo micronucleus assay (up to 500 mg/kg, highest concentration tested). No carcinogenicity studies were conducted with artesunate and none are proposed. Given the short duration of treatment and negative findings from the genotoxic test battery, carcinogenicity studies are not indicated to support submission of an NDA for IV artesunate.
L2913: Over 15 reproductive and developmental toxicity studies with artesunate have been submitted to the IND, including GLP-compliant embryofetal toxicity studies of artesunate conducted in rat, rabbit and monkey and embryofetal development studies with an oral combination of chlorproguanil/dapsone/artesunate (CDA) in rat and rabbit. Nine of the submitted reproductive and
L2914: developmental toxicity studies were previously reviewed3. In summary, artesunate was embryo- or fetal-toxic in all animal models tested and induced an increased number of embryo or fetal deaths and abortions compared with control animals. Main developmental findings were cardiovascular malformations (ventricular septal defects) and skeletal anomalies (ribs, long bones, and/or scapulae). These development effects were dependent on both the dose level and the day or days of administration during gestation, although NOAELs were not determined for either the fetal rat or rabbit. Artesunate also was toxic to several hematologic cell types in cynomolgus monkeys and to monkey nucleated fetal red blood cells *in vitro*. Maternal findings of weight loss and/or reduced appetite were not significant, apart from weight loss and lack of appetite following treatment with the combination product Chlorproguanil/Dapsone/Artesunate. The Sponsor noted that no ICH compliant, GLP studies assess the effects on artesunate on male or female fertility or pre-and post-natal development of offspring exposed to drug via the dams. The submitted nonclinical studies in rat did not examine the effects of artesunate on male reproductive organs, and the 14-day repeat dose study in dog showed no effect on male reproductive organs, though the pathology report notes animals were not yet sexually mature at time of necropsy (animals were 5.5 to 6.5 months old). The Sponsor provided a summary of the published literature indicating adverse effects of artesunate and other artemisinin derived compounds on spermatogenesis in male rats and mice (e.g., abnormal sperm, decreased sperm count).
L2915: The Sponsor submitted a juvenile rat study performed with the combination product Chlorproguanil/Dapsone/Artesunate (CDA) administered during day 4 to 31 postpartum. Levels of artesunate in the CDA combination product were 0.235 and 0.706 mg/kg. Study results indicate no drug-related effects on clinical signs, body weights, hematology, or macroscopic necropsy observation at any tested dose (no histopathology data was available). The Sponsor states that based on other study findings, the toxicity of the CDA combination product is largely attributable to the artesunate content, and that any toxicity in the juvenile rat with CDA is representative of the toxicity with artesunate alone. Of note, previous dose-range finding studies conducted with CDA combination at higher doses (2.35 – 23.5 mg/kg artesunate) was associated with high rate of male and female pup mortality, resulting in early termination of treatment. The cause of pup death was not determined.
L2916: *Reviewer comment:* The submitted and proposed safety pharmacology studies and 4-week repeat-dose toxicity study with 28-day recovery period are in accordance with M3(R2) and ICHS7A and would support marketing authorization.
L2917: It is recommended that at a minimum, an audited draft of the study report from the repeat-dose toxicity studies be available at time of NDA submission. Having the findings from this study available during NDA review will allow for assessment of any unexpected toxicities with the semi-synthetic API formulation, whether any additional nonclinical studies will be needed to bridge any reproductive safety and toxicity concerns not addressed in the clinical data and evaluate any need for juvenile toxicity studies. It is not clear from the meeting package when the Sponsor will have the semi-synthetic API available to begin conducting the 4-week repeat dose studies. Per communications with the CMC review team, it appears that the semi-synthetic API described as being manufactured in might not have been released yet. The project manager, Gregory DiBernardo, spoke with the Sponsor on 2/6/2019 regarding the status of their pre-NDA CMC only meeting request for this IND and was told the Sponsor plans to submit their NDA during the first week of May 2019, at which time the Sponsor
L2918: 3
L2919: https://darrts.fda.gov//darrts/faces/ViewDocument?documentId=090140af800c69eb&amp;_afrRedirect=4658590351298442
L2920: anticipates having 3 months stability data and side-by-side comparative analysis between the (b) (4) and semi-synthetic API. It is recommended that the Sponsor begin conducting the 4-week repeat dose studies as soon as possible. Provided they have sufficient quantities of the semi-synthetic API on hand, a draft report of the study findings could conceivably be made available to the Agency within the Sponsor's projected time frame of NDA submission.
L2921: ## 4. LIST OF SPONSOR-PROVIDED QUESTIONS FOR DISCUSSION
L2922: ## Question 1:
L2923: A comprehensive pharmacology, pharmacokinetic, and toxicology program has been undertaken to characterize the non-clinical efficacy, disposition, metabolism, and safety of AS. A tabular summary of nonclinical studies submitted to IND 64,769 are provided in Table 4 to Table 6 (Section 2.12.1.1). Does the Agency agree that the existing toxicology studies and the newly proposed studies (Table 7) including 4-week repeat dose rat and dog toxicity studies as per ICH M3(R2) and transporter protein studies as per FDA 2017 with the semi-synthetic drug substance is sufficient to support the NDA filing?
L2924: Pharmacology/Toxicology Response: Your proposed pharmacology, pharmacokinetic, and toxicology program using the nonclinical studies submitted to IND 64769 and newly proposed 4-week repeat-dose toxicity study in rat (including neurobehavioral assessment) and dog with 28-day recovery, along with *in vitro* transporter protein studies appears reasonable. Adequacy of the newly proposed nonclinical studies to support the NDA filing will be assessed upon review of the study reports at the time of submission.
L2925: ## Question 2:
L2926: Amivas is acutely aware that an urgent unmet medical need exists with IV quinidine being withdrawn from the US market. Can Amivas file the current non-clinical (and clinical) data package for review in January 2019 with the clock starting for NDA review as soon as the CMC package is received by the Agency in April 2019?
L2927: Pharmacology/Toxicology Response: PharmTox is fine with a rolling review.
L2928: ## Question 3:
L2929: If the answers to Q1 and Q2 are yes, can Amivas file the 4-week dog and 4-week rat studies as a post-marketing commitment? Our view is that the proposed in-vitro transporter studies (Section 2.12.1.4) are useful scientifically to support the clinical data we have with severe renally and hepatically impaired ICU patients, but the 4 week repeated dose toxicity studies to comply with ICH M3(R2) will not substantially add to the existing evidence provided in the 3-day rat, 7-day monkey and 14-day dog studies (Table 6) and could be deferred.
L2930: ## Pharmacology/Toxicology:
L2931: - Per ICH Guideline M3(R2), repeated-dose toxicity studies of one-month duration are recommended to support marketing of an indicated treatment up to 2 weeks (b) (4) (b) (4) (b) (4) We recommend commencing the 4-week repeat dose studies in rat and dog as soon as the semi-synthetic API becomes available. We are willing to review audited draft reports of the study findings at time of NDA submission, with the final full study reports to be submitted within 60 days of the NDA submission. Any changes in the final study report from the audited draft should be clearly identified.
L2932: - Plan to provide a rationale for your maximum intended dosing levels and/or projected multiples of human exposure in the repeated-dose toxicity studies. Ideally, the studies should be conducted in a clinically relevant manner (i.e., IV) and the highest dose in a general toxicology study should demonstrate the maximum tolerated dose (MTD), achieve large exposure multiples above the clinical dose, or reach the maximum feasible dose (MFD).
L2933: ## **Question 4:**
L2934: The existing toxicology package with additional support from published studies have been used to identify risks in terms of potential effects on fertility and post-natal development. There is a potential for effect on male fertility, and at clinical doses, there is an apparent lack of effect on postnatal development and maturation (Section 2.12.1.3). The sponsor proposes no further reproductive nonclinical studies are needed. Does the Agency agree?
L2935: **Pharmacology/Toxicology Response:** The adequacy existing reproductive nonclinical studies using the API to support your NDA submission using the semi-synthetic API will be evaluated upon review of your 4-week repeat dose with 4-week recovery period toxicology studies in rat and dog. You may wish to consider including a sperm analysis (sperm counts, sperm motility, sperm morphology) and evaluation of male and female reproductive organs as part of the planned repeat dose toxicity studies. Typically, pre-and-post natal development studies are recommended to support marketing authorization in accordance with ICH Guidelines M3(R2) and S5A. Additional pre- and post-natal developmental toxicity assessments may be needed to address any unexpected toxicities encountered in your study findings and to bridge any reproductive safety and toxicity concerns not addressed in the clinical data.
L2936: ## **Question 5:**
L2937: Studies in adolescent to adult aged animals across species are provided by the sponsor's toxicology program, including short single/3-day studies in Sprague Dawley (SD) rats, 14-day studies in beagle dogs, and 7-day dosing in rhesus monkeys. A juvenile rat study with a combination product including AS did not show adverse effects in the developing target organs affected by AS in the older animals studied under Good Laboratory Practice (GLP) (Section 2.12.1.2). Amivas does not consider additional juvenile animal studies warranted or likely to add to the current clinical understanding of AS toxicity in pediatric patients. The sponsor does not intend to perform additional non-clinical juvenile studies to support short-term dosing in infants/children with severe malaria as part of the NDA submission. Does the Agency agree?
L2938: ## **Pharmacology/Toxicology Response:**
L2939: The conduct of any juvenile animal toxicity studies should be considered when previous animal data and human safety data, including effects from other drugs of the pharmacological class, are judged to be insufficient to support pediatric studies. Based on the studies completed to date, additional non-clinical juvenile studies to support short-term dosing in infants/children with severe malaria as part of the NDA submission may not be needed. However, a determination of the safety and efficacy of IV artesunate for treatment of malaria in children will be determined upon review of the completed 1-month GLP repeat-dose toxicology studies with a 28-day recovery period in rat (with functional observational battery) and dog using the semisynthetic drug formulation, and clinical findings. Adequacy of these findings, and identification of the need for juvenile toxicity studies, will be assessed upon review of the final study reports and clinical trial data at the time of submission.
L2940: ## 5. REVIEW OF SUBMITTED STUDIES
L2941: ## 5.1 Safety Pharmacology
L2942: ## 5.1.1 Central nervous system
L2943: **Study Title:** The Neuropathology of Intravenous Artesunate Administration in the Rhesus monkey (*Macaca mulatta*) (b) (4) Pathology Report PN02-01)
L2944: **Methods:** Rhesus monkeys aged 5-25 years (n=2 of each sex/group) were administered once per day with IV artelinate lysine salt (AL; 5.9, 11.8, and 47.2 mg/kg), IV artesunate sodium salt (AS; 4, 8, 16, 32, and 128 mg/kg) for seven days. Lysine and sodium bicarbonate served as vehicle controls for AL and AS, respectively (n=2, one male and one female). An additional group treated intramuscularly once per day with 16 mg/kg arteether (AE) served as positive control for neurotoxicity assessment. Animals were euthanized 14 days after the last treatment and perfused with Bouin's fluid.
L2945: **Results:** Animals treated with AE exhibited chronic histological findings consistent with widespread neuronal necrosis in brain stem nuclei. Two females treated with 128 mg/kg AS died of GI/hematologic toxicity prior to perfusion fixation and were noted unsuitable for study given the widespread cellular damage which prevented detailed neuroanatomic analysis. One male in the 128 mg/kg AS group was prematurely euthanized on day 10 due to impending death with multi-organ failure, including neurologic effects. No evidence of neuronal necrosis was found in animals administered AL or in the surviving animals administered AS.
L2946: **Reviewer comment:** This study was not conducted in compliance with GLP regulations. Study report was submitted under Safety Pharmacology, but focused on histologic lesions in the medulla oblongata, pons, cerebellum, midbrain, auditory and visual systems in the brainstem; neurobehavioral assessments of the animals were not provided.
L2947: ## 5.1.2 Cardiovascular and Respiratory system
L2948: **Study Title:** Cardiovascular Safety Pharmacology Study of Artesunate in Beagle Dogs (Study Report 1466-103).
L2949: **Methods:** Telemetered beagle dogs aged 6-7 months (2 each per sex) were administered sterile 0.3M phosphate buffer (vehicle control) via intravenous injection on study day 1. Beginning study day 2, animals received a dose of 10, 20, and 50 mg/kg via a single slow intravenous dose and 50 mg/kg administered via a single fast intravenous dose with at least a one-week wash out period between each escalating dose level.
L2950: **Results:** Artesunate (single slow IV dose of 0, 10, 20 and 50 mg/kg or single fast IV dose of 50 mg/kg) did not have any effects on mortality, clinical observations, body weights, ECGs, QT intervals, heart rate, blood pressure, or respiratory rate.
L2951: **Reviewer comment:** The study was conducted in compliance with GLP regulations. Artesunate acid (Batch No. 14290-16A; (b) (4), \ge 99.5\% purity) was dissolved in phosphate buffer prior to use.

L2957: ## Observations and Results
L2958: ## Mortality (≥ 2 times/day)
L2959: All animals survived until scheduled termination.
L2960: ## Clinical Signs (prior to dose on SD1, weekly thereafter, and at termination)
L2961: No changes in clinical observations (evaluation of skin and fur characteristics, eye and mucus membranes, respiratory, circulatory, autonomic and central nervous systems, somatomotor and behavior patterns) were observed between artesunate-treated animals and vehicle control. Some animals exhibited erythema, diarrhea, emesis, and mucoid feces. These responses were not considered to be test-article related as they not show a dose-dependent pattern, and 2 of the 4 control-treated animals also experienced erythema.
L2962: ## **Body Weights (prior to dose on SD1, weekly thereafter, and at termination)**
L2963: No effects on body weights were observed in animals administered artesunate.
L2964: ## **Feed Consumption (daily)**
L2965: No significant differences in mean food consumption between artesunate and control-treated animals were observed.
L2966: ## **ECG (pre-initial dose, ~2h post-dose on SD1, SD2, SD8 and SD15)**
L2967: No atrioventricular conduction defects or premature atrial or ventricular complexes were observed in artesunate or control-treated animals.
L2968: ## **Hematology (prior to initial dosing, SD15 and prior to necropsy)**
L2969: No changes in coagulation parameters were observed between artesunate and control-treated animals at the end of treatment (SD15) or at the end of the recovery period (SD29).
L2970: ## **Clinical Chemistry (prior to initial dosing, SD15 and prior to necropsy)**

L2974: In summary, females treated with 10 mg/kg/day artesunate showed higher WBC, with corresponding increase in absolute lymphocyte and monocyte counts. Females treated with 20 mg/kg/day artesunate
L2975: 4 Adapted from pages 191-210 of Study Report 1466-102
L2976: also showed significantly elevated absolute lymphocyte counts compared with control treated animals. A significant reduction in RBC, HCT, and RETIC was observed in females treated with \ge 20 mg/kg/day artesunate. A significant reduction in HGB was also observed in females treated with artesunate at doses \ge 35 mg/kg/day. Males treated with artesunate also showed had lower levels of RBC, HBG, HCT and RETIC, with the reduction in RETIC reaching statistical significance at \ge 35 mg/kg/day. By the end of recovery period (SD29), no statistically significant differences in clinical chemistry parameters were observed in artesunate-treated animals compared with the control group.
L2977: Variation in sodium levels in the animals prior to treatment were observed: females assigned to 20 and 35 mg/kg day and males assigned to 35 mg/kg/day had higher sodium compared with animals assigned to the control group. On SD15, significantly higher amounts of sodium and chloride were observed in females and males, respectively, treated with 50 mg/kg/day artesunate. A decrease in aspartate aminotransferase was observed in males treated with 10 mg/kg/day. These changes were attributed to animal variation and are not considered to be test article-related.
L2978: ## Gross Pathology
L2979: Gross pathology included examination of external surface of the body, injection site, all orifices, cranial, thoracic and abdominal cavities and their contents. Discoloration of the injection site and enlarged testes were observed in both control and artesunate-treated animals and discoloration of the cecum was observed in 1 of 4 males dosed with 20 mg/kg/day artesunate. These findings were considered to be unrelated to test article administration.
L2980: ## Organ Weights
L2981: There were no statistically significant differences in mean absolute organ weights, mean organ-to-body weight rations, or mean organ-to-brain weight rations between artesunate and control-treated animals.
L2982: ## Histopathology
L2983: Adequate Battery: Yes
L2984: Peer Review: Yes
L2985: Follicular lymphoid hyperplasia of the mandibular lymph nodes and minimal to mild hemorrhage/inflammatory responses were observed across control and treated animals. No test-article related effects on histopathology were observed at end of treatment (SD15) or recovery (SD29).
L2986: ## Toxicokinetics
L2987: Blood was collected (jugular vein) for toxicokinetic evaluation at the following timepoints:

L2993: Reviewer comment: Ophthalmoscopic evaluations and uranalysis were not performed in this study. Toxicokinetic results were summarized in a separate study report, No. 1446-102 (PK Report).
L2994: ## 5.2.2 Study Title: Toxicity of Subacute, 7-Day Dosing of Candidate Intravenous Artemisinins in Rhesus Monkey
L2995: Study no.:	AQ0008-02-AF
Study report location:	FDR
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Duration:	Seven days
Duration Units:	days
GLP compliance:	N
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	Artesunate, Batch No. 010601
L2996: ## Methods
L2997: Doses:	0, 4, 8, 16, 32, and 128 mg/kg
Frequency of dosing:	Daily
Number/Sex/Group:	2 each male and female/artesunate group
1 each male and female/control group
Dose volume:	Not specified
Formulation/Vehicle:	Sodium bicarbonate (5%)
Route of administration:	INTRAVENOUS
Species:	MONKEY
Strain:	RHESUS
Age / Sexual Maturity:	4 to 25 years
Dosing Solution Analysis:	Not provided
L2998: ## Observations and Result
L2999: ## Mortality (3x/day)
L3000: All animals in the control, 4, 8, 16, and 32 mg/kg/day artesunate groups survived until the end of the study. Three of the 4 animals administered 128 mg/kg artesunate died prematurely. Two of the animals died overnight (on days 6 and 10) and the third showed signs of transient blindness, ptosis, and nystagmus prior to elective euthanasia with Bouin's fixation on day 10. Prostration was not noted initially but was noted as a pre-terminal event in all of the animals that died.
L3001: ## Clinical Signs (3x/day during dosing period, 2x/day during recovery period)
L3002: Depressed motor activity was observed in animals administered \ge 8 mg/kg artesunate, with severity increasing with increasing dose. The slight depressed motor activity observed in one each of the animals treated with 8 and 16 mg/kg was transient in nature (resolving within 1h), whereas 3 of the 4 animals treated with 32 mg/kg displayed mild-to-moderate decreased motor activity that was more persistent (up to 6h). All animals in the high dose group (128 mg/kg) showed persistent depressed motor activity, and 3 of the 4 animals displayed slight truncal ataxia. Hypersalivation, prostration and ataxia were observed in animals administered \ge 32 mg/kg artesunate. Soft stool and diarrhea were observed in animals administered \ge 16 mg/kg. The GI distress resolved in the 16 and 32 mg/kg groups, whereas diarrhea in the 128 mg/kg treated animals was more severe and progressed to bloody mucosa.
L3003: ## Body Weights (Days 1, 4, and 7 prior to dosing and Days 10, 12, 15, and 22 during recovery period)
L3004: A weight loss of 5-10% occurred in animals treated with artesunate, with the exception of one animal administered 16 mg/kg that had an 11.3% weight loss.
L3005: ## Feed Consumption
L3006: One female in the 8 mg/kg group displayed intermittent poor appetite during the study period. The appetite of the animals in the higher dose groups were more severely affected: 2 of 4 animals in the 16 and 32 mg/kg group displaying poor appetite, requiring NG feeds up to SD22; poor appetite with severe anorexia requiring IV fluids occurred in all the animals administered 128 mg/kg artesunate.
L3007: ## Hematology and Clinical Chemistry (1 day prior to administration and Days 4, 8, 15, and at terminal sacrifice on day 22)
L3008: Significant leukopenia, neutropenia, red blood cell loss, and reticulocytopenia was observed in all animals administered artesunate, with thrombocytopenia observed at doses \ge 32 mg/kg. The leukocyte,
L3009: neutrophil, RBC, and reticulocyte numbers rebounded during the recovery period (between days 15-22). One animal in the control group had a 5% loss of RBC at time of euthanasia, which was attributed to phlebotomy. No other hematologic changes were observed in the control group.
L3010: A significant total drop in protein (10-15%) was observed in animals treated with \ge 8 mg/kg artesunate. An increase in alkaline phosphate was observed starting at 16 mg/kg, and animals in the higher dose groups (32 and 128 mg/kg) also had an increase in BUN, this increase was statistically significant in the 128 mg/kg group.
L3011: ## Urinalysis (baseline and days 8, 15, and 22 after start of administration)
L3012: Microscopic hemoglobinuria was noted in at least 50% of animals treated with 8, 16, and 32 mg/kg artesunate on more than one occasion. The microscopic hemoglobinuria resolved by SD22. Urine from animals in the high dose group (128 mg/kg) was red, contained bilirubin or urobilirubin. The three animals that died prematurely had ketonuria, hemoglobinuria, pyuria and proteinuria.
L3013: ## Gross Pathology and Histopathology
L3014: Necropsies in the control, 4, 8, 16 and 32 mg/kg were unrevealing for brain or other organ abnormalities. Animals who died prematurely had hypocellular marrow consistent with the peripheral neutropenia and reticulocytopenia. Hepatic vacuolation was observed the 3 animals that died but was not observed in the surviving animal. Moderate-to-severe congestion and hemorrhage of the stomach and intestines were also observed, and 2 of the 3 animals that died had moderate adrenocortical congestion and hemorrhage. The males in the 128 mg/kg group had noted spermatid degeneration. The surviving animal showed neuronal chromatolysis in the cranial nerve nucleus.
L3015: *Reviewer comment:* The protocol states that the adequate battery of tissues was collected and examined, with complete gross and microscopic analysis to be carried out by a board-certified veterinary pathologist at [REDACTED](b) (4) but detailed histopathological findings were not provided in this study report. The pathology report containing histopathologic findings in the brain of animals treated with artesunate was submitted as [REDACTED](b) (4) Pathology Report PN02-01 under Safety Pharmacology and is discussed in detail under 5.1.1 above. The protocol notes that lumbar puncture for CSF was performed on day 7 (following treatment), and that blood was taken for at baseline and at days 1, 2, 4, and 9 after dosing for assessment of antimalarial activity. Activity levels in plasma were reported to be \le LOQ and no antimalarial activity was detected in the CSF.
L3016: ## 5.3 Genetic Toxicology
L3017: ## 5.3.1 *In Vitro* Reverse Mutation Assay in Bacterial Cells (Ames)
L3018: Study Title: Evaluation of Artesunic Acid in the *Salmonella-Escherichia coli*/microsome plate incorporation assay
L3019: Study no.: G305-08
Study report location: FDR
Conducting laboratory and location: [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L3020: Methods

GLP compliance: Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity: Artesunic acid, Lot 14462-16, purity 100%

Strains: *S. typhimurium* TA1535, TA1537, TA98, TA100 and *E. coli* strain WP2 (*uvrA*)

Concentrations in definitive study: 156.3, 312.5, 325, 1250, 2500, and 5000 mcg/plate

Basis of concentration selection: The highest dose (5000 mcg/plate) is the recommended maximum testing concentration. Dose selection was made to assess potential dose-response relationship and at least one cytotoxic dose.

Negative control: DMSO

Positive control: (-S9) TA1535, TA100: Sodium azide (5 mcg/50 mcl)
TA1537: 9-Aminoacridine hydrochloride hydrate (50 mcg/50 mcl)
TA98: 2-Nitrofluorene (5 mcg/50 mcl)
WP2 (*uvrA*): 4-Nitroquinoline N-oxide (2.5 mcg/50 mcl)
(+S9) 2-Anthramine:
2 mcg/mcl (TA98, TA100)
4 mcg/50 mcl (TA1535, TA1537)
20 mcg/50 mcl (WP2 (*uvrA*))

Formulation/Vehicle: Artesunic Acid/DMSO

Incubation &amp; sampling time: Plates were incubated at 37°C for 48h

Comment on Study Validity: Experiment was considered valid: strains were analyzed to confirm genetic markers, solvent controls were within historical limits for spontaneous revertants, positive controls elicited positive response and there were at least three nontoxic dose levels.
L3021: ## Results
L3022: Artesunic Acid was negative in the bacterial reverse mutation assay under test conditions used in this study. Cytotoxicity, apparent by either decreased revertant counts, thinning of the background lawn, or the complete absence of revertant counts, was seen only with the *Salmonella* tester strains at 5000 mcg/plate in the presence and absence of metabolic activation. No statistically significant increases in the number of revertant colonies were seen for *S. typhimurium* TA1535, TA1537, TA98 or *E. coli* strain WP2(*uvrA*) in the presence or absence of metabolic activation (10% S9). A slight (&lt;2-fold) but dose-dependent significant increase in revertant colonies was observed in TA100 in the presence of 10% S9. However, the increase in revertant colonies was not considered to be toxicologically significant as the number of colonies fell within the historical spontaneous range for the strain.

L3028: significant effect on mitotic index or confluency (\ge 50\% reduction).
L3029: ## Results
L3030: Artesunic acid was negative in the *in vitro* mammalian chromosome aberration assay under test conditions used in this study. No concentration-related increases or statistically significant increases in structural chromosomal aberrations were observed at any dose level in the presence or absence of metabolic activation.
L3031: ## 5.3.3 *In Vivo* Clastogenicity Assay in Rodent (Micronucleus Assay)

L3036: ## Results
L3037: Artesunic Acid was negative in the mammalian bone marrow erythrocyte micronucleus assay under test conditions. No statistically significant increases in the frequency of micronucleated polychromatic erythrocytes (PCE) was observed at any dose level in either male or female mice at 24- or 48-hr post treatment.

L3050: Diagrammatic representation of the mechanism of action and resistance to qinghaosu/artemisinin.
The mechanism of action involves:
Artemisinins
Activation by Fe^{2+}
Generation of free radicals, hydroperoxides, electrophilic intermediates
Alkylation, oxidation, and hydroxylation of parasite target biomolecules
Damage of parasite proteins
This damage leads to:
Rapid killing (for Artemisinin-sensitive parasite)
Slow clearance (for Artemisinin-sensitive parasite)
Artemisinin-resistant parasite (via Up-regulation of unfolded protein response pathways (e.g., increased reactive oxidative stress complex)).

L3053: Flowchart illustrating patient disposition in the AQUAMAT trial (Per Protocol analysis) regarding neurological sequelae at discharge and 28 days.
Initial Cohort: 191 patients with possible neurological deficits at discharge (110 artesunate, 81 quinine).
Exclusions: 21 excluded (11 artesunate, 10 quinine). Reasons for exclusion include: 4 unspecified behavioural problems, 9 no neurological problem confirmed, 4 pre-existing neurological problems, and 4 mild motor impairment in young children <18 months.
Remaining Cohort: 170 patients with neurological sequelae at discharge (99 artesunate, 71 quinine).
Follow-up Data: 41 lost to follow-up at day 28 (23 artesunate, 18 quinine). 68 sequelae resolved by day 28 (42 artesunate, 26 quinine).
Final Cohort: 61 patients with neurological sequelae at day 28 (34 artesunate, 27 quinine).
Sequelae Distribution at Day 28:
10 mild neurological sequelae at day 28 (8 artesunate, 2 quinine)
8 moderate neurological sequelae at day 28 (3 artesunate, 5 quinine)
43 severe neurological sequelae at day 28* (23 artesunate, 20 quinine; 30 severe motor impairment, 14 cortical blindness, 9 severe speech or hearing impairment)
*Some patients had severe impairment in more than one domain.
L3054: Figure 18-1 displays two graphs showing mean plasma concentration-time profiles. The Y-axis for both graphs is Plasma concentration (ng/ml) on a logarithmic scale (1 to 100,000). The X-axis for both graphs is Time after start of infusion (h) (0 to 7).
The top graph is labeled AS (Artesunate). The bottom graph is labeled DHA (Dihydroartemisinin).
Both graphs show five dose cohorts (0.5, 1, 2, 4, and 8 mg/kg). Higher doses generally result in higher peak concentrations and longer duration of detectable plasma concentration.
AS Graph (Top): Dose cohorts are 0.5 mg/kg (lowest peak), 1.0 mg/kg, 2.0 mg/kg, 4.0 mg/kg, and 8.0 mg/kg (highest peak). Concentrations decrease rapidly, reaching near zero by 4 hours for the 0.5 mg/kg dose and remaining detectable up to 7 hours for the 8.0 mg/kg dose.
DHA Graph (Bottom): Dose cohorts are 0.5 mg/kg (lowest peak), 1.0 mg/kg, 2.0 mg/kg, 4.0 mg/kg, and 8.0 mg/kg (highest peak). Concentrations decrease rapidly, reaching near zero by 4 hours for the 0.5 mg/kg dose and remaining detectable up to 7 hours for the 8.0 mg/kg dose.

L3061: Figure 18-8 is a scatter plot showing the simulated total first dose exposures (AUC_{0-12h}) of Dihydroartemisinin (DHA) for children across various body weights (6 kg to 24 kg). The Y-axis represents AUC_{0-12h} in units of h \times ng/ml, ranging from 2,000 to 5,000. The X-axis represents Body weight (kg), ranging from 6 to 24.
The plot compares two dosing regimens:
Standard dosing (black circles and error bars).
Adjusted dosing regimen proposed in Hendriksen et al. (red circles and error bars).
For each body weight, the solid circle represents the median exposure, and the error bars indicate the 25th and 75th percentiles of 1,000 simulations. A dashed horizontal line indicates the median exposure for the 25 kg weight group after the standard 2.4 mg/kg dose.
The data shows that the adjusted dosing regimen (red) generally achieves higher median exposures than the standard dosing regimen (black) across the weight range, particularly for lighter children (6 kg to 10 kg), where the adjusted regimen is significantly higher. For heavier children (16 kg to 24 kg), the exposures are similar between the two regimens.

L3084: Figure 18-30 displays two line graphs comparing Hemoglobin (g/dL) and Hematocrit (%) between the Artesunate Pooled group (blue line) and the Placebo group (orange line) across six study visits (1: SCREENING, 2: INPATIENT, 4: DAY 4, 5: DAY 8, 6: DAY 15). Error bars represent Mean \pm SE.
Left Graph: Parameter: Hemoglobin (g/dL)
The Artesunate Pooled group shows a steady decrease in hemoglobin from approximately 14.5 g/dL at Screening to approximately 13.7 g/dL at Day 15. The Placebo group shows a fluctuating hemoglobin level, starting around 14.9 g/dL, peaking around 15.0 g/dL at Day 4, and ending around 14.7 g/dL at Day 15.
Right Graph: Parameter: Hematocrit (%)
The Artesunate Pooled group shows a steady decrease in hematocrit from approximately 42.5% at Screening to approximately 40.0% at Day 8, and then slightly increases to approximately 40.2% at Day 15. The Placebo group shows a fluctuating hematocrit level, starting around 43.8%, peaking around 43.9% at Day 4, and ending around 42.5% at Day 15.
L3085: Figure 18-31 is a line graph showing Reticulocyte Count (Parameter: Reticulocytes (10^6/L)) by Study Visit (Analysis Visit) for Pooled Artesunate versus Placebo. The Y-axis represents Analysis Value: Mean\pmSE, ranging from 27,500 to 67,500. The X-axis represents Analysis Visit (1: SCREENING, 2: INPATIENT, 4: DAY 4, 5: DAY 8, 6: DAY 15).
The graph compares two groups: Artesunate Pooled (blue line) and Placebo (orange line).
At Screening, both groups have high reticulocyte counts (Artesunate Pooled around 62,000; Placebo around 52,000). Following infusion (Inpatient visit), both counts drop significantly. By Day 4, the Artesunate Pooled count drops to approximately 32,000, while the Placebo count drops to approximately 47,000. By Day 8, the Artesunate Pooled count rises to approximately 59,000, and the Placebo count rises to approximately 49,000. By Day 15, the Artesunate Pooled count is around 60,000, and the Placebo count is around 48,000. Error bars represent Mean\pmSE.
L3086: Figure 18-32 is a line graph showing Reticulocyte Count (Parameter: Reticulocytes (10^6/L)) by Study Visit (Analysis Visit) for different dose regimens of Artesunate and Placebo. The Y-axis represents Analysis Value: Mean\pmSE, ranging from 10,000 to 120,000. The X-axis represents Analysis Visit (1: SCREENING, 2: INPATIENT, 4: DAY 4, 5: DAY 8, 6: DAY 15).
The graph compares seven groups: Artesunate 0.5 mg/kg (blue), Artesunate 1.0 mg/kg (orange), Artesunate 2.0 mg/kg (green), Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg (red), Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg (purple), and Placebo (dark red).
At Screening, reticulocyte counts are high across all groups (Artesunate 0.5 mg/kg around 60,000; Artesunate 1.0 mg/kg around 50,000; Artesunate 2.0 mg/kg around 78,000; Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg around 65,000; Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg around 55,000; Placebo around 55,000). Following infusion (Inpatient visit), all groups show a significant drop in reticulocyte count. By Day 4, the lowest count is observed for Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg (around 20,000), followed by Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg (around 25,000). By Day 8, counts generally rise, with Artesunate 2.0 mg/kg reaching around 70,000. By Day 15, counts are generally stable or slightly increased compared to Day 8, with Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg reaching around 90,000. Error bars represent Mean\pmSE.
L3087: Figure 18-33 is a line graph showing Neutrophil Counts (10^9/L) by Study Visit (1: SCREENING, 2: INPATIENT, 4: DAY 4, 5: DAY 8, 6: DAY 15) for various Artesunate dose regimens (0.5 mg/kg, 1.0 mg/kg, 2.0 mg/kg, 4.0 mg/kg, 8.0 mg/kg) compared to Placebo. The Y-axis ranges from 3.00 to 4.75. The graph illustrates that neutrophil counts generally decrease from screening/inpatient visits to Day 4, and then increase towards Day 15. The decrease is most pronounced for the highest dose regimens (4.0 and 8.0 mg/kg).
Legend:
Artesunate 0.5 mg/kg (Blue line with triangles)
Artesunate 1.0 mg/kg (Orange line with squares)
Artesunate 2.0 mg/kg (Green line with circles)
Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg (Red line with diamonds)
Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg (Purple line with crosses)
Placebo (Brown line with plus signs)
L3088: Figure 18-34 displays two line graphs comparing pooled Artesunate versus Placebo across study visits (1: SCREENING, 2: INPATIENT, 4: DAY 4, 5: DAY 8, 6: DAY 15).
The left graph shows Alanine Aminotransferase (U/L). The right graph shows Aspartate Aminotransferase (U/L). Both graphs show that Alanine Aminotransferase levels increase significantly in the Artesunate Pooled group by Day 8 and Day 15, while Aspartate Aminotransferase levels remain relatively stable across all visits for both groups.
Legend (Applies to both graphs):
Artesunate Pooled (Blue line with triangles)
Placebo (Orange line with squares)
L3089: Figure 18-35 is a line graph showing Hematocrit (%) (Y-axis, 35 to 48) versus Analysis Timepoint for Reporting (X-axis, 3: DAY 0 to 99: REPEAT/UNSCHEDULED). The parameter is Hematocrit (%). The graph compares Artesunate Pooled (blue triangles) and Placebo (orange circles) groups. Both groups show a transient decrease in hematocrit following Day 1 (4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR), with the Placebo group dropping significantly below the Artesunate group by Day 7 (8: DAY 7). The Placebo group then shows a sharp increase, reaching approximately 47.5% by Day 15 (10: DAY 15). The Artesunate group remains relatively stable, ending around 39%.

The parameter is Hemoglobin (g/dL). The graph compares Artesunate Pooled (blue triangles) and Placebo (orange circles) groups. Both groups show a transient decrease in hemoglobin following Day 1 (4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR), with the Placebo group dropping significantly below the Artesunate group by Day 7 (8: DAY 7). The Placebo group then shows a sharp increase, reaching approximately 15.8 g/dL by Day 15 (10: DAY 15). The Artesunate group remains relatively stable, ending around 13.5 g/dL.
L3091: Figure 18-37 is a line graph showing Platelets (10^9/L) (Y-axis, 175 to 325) versus Analysis Timepoint for Reporting (X-axis, 3: DAY 0 to 99: REPEAT/UNSCHEDULED). The parameter is Platelets (10^9/L). The graph compares Artesunate Pooled (blue triangles) and Placebo (orange circles) groups. Both groups show a transient decrease in platelets following Day 1 (4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR), with the Artesunate group dropping significantly below the Placebo group by Day 7 (8: DAY 7). The Artesunate group then shows a sharp increase, reaching approximately 290 10^9/L by Day 15 (10: DAY 15). The Placebo group remains relatively stable, ending around 270 10^9/L.

L3094: Figure 18-40 displays two line graphs comparing Alanine Aminotransferase (ALT) and Aspartate Aminotransferase (AST) levels across study visits for pooled Artesunate and Placebo groups.
Left Graph: Parameter: Alanine Aminotransferase (U/L)
The Y-axis represents Analysis Value: Mean\pmSE (ranging from 10 to 70). The X-axis represents Analysis Timepoint for Reporting (3: DAY 0, 4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR, 5: POST DOSE 2 6 HOUR, 6: POST DOSE 3 6 HOUR, 7: DAY 4, 8: DAY 7, 9: DAY 10, 10: DAY 15, 99: REPEAT/UNSCHEDULED).
Legend: Artesunate Pooled (blue line with triangles), Placebo (orange line with circles).
Both groups show ALT levels generally below 20 U/L until Day 7. Artesunate Pooled shows a transient peak around Day 7 (approx. 58 U/L) and a subsequent drop, while Placebo levels remain relatively stable (around 20-25 U/L).
Right Graph: Parameter: Aspartate Aminotransferase (U/L)
The Y-axis represents Analysis Value: Mean\pmSE (ranging from 15.0 to 42.5). The X-axis represents Analysis Timepoint for Reporting (3: DAY 0, 4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR, 5: POST DOSE 2 6 HOUR, 6: POST DOSE 3 6 HOUR, 7: DAY 4, 8: DAY 7, 9: DAY 10, 10: DAY 15, 99: REPEAT/UNSCHEDULED).
Legend: Artesunate Pooled (blue line with triangles), Placebo (orange line with circles).
Both groups show AST levels generally below 25 U/L until Day 7. Artesunate Pooled shows a transient peak around Day 7 (approx. 38 U/L) and a subsequent drop, while Placebo levels remain relatively stable (around 20-25 U/L).
L3095: Figure 18-41 displays two line graphs comparing Alanine Aminotransferase (ALT) and Aspartate Aminotransferase (AST) levels across study visits for Artesunate at three doses (2.0 mg/kg, 4.0 mg/kg, 8.0 mg/kg) and Placebo.
Left Graph: Parameter: Alanine Aminotransferase (U/L)
The Y-axis represents Analysis Value: Mean\pmSE (ranging from 0 to 125). The X-axis represents Analysis Timepoint for Reporting (3: DAY 0, 4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR, 5: POST DOSE 2 6 HOUR, 6: POST DOSE 3 6 HOUR, 7: DAY 4, 8: DAY 7, 9: DAY 10, 10: DAY 15, 99: REPEAT/UNSCHEDULED).
Legend: Artesunate 2.0 mg/kg (blue line with triangles), Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg (orange line with circles), Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg (green line with diamonds), Placebo (red line with squares).
Placebo levels remain low (around 10-20 U/L). Artesunate groups show a transient peak around Day 7 (Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg peaks highest, around 90 U/L), followed by a drop.
Right Graph: Parameter: Aspartate Aminotransferase (U/L)
The Y-axis represents Analysis Value: Mean\pmSE (ranging from 15 to 60). The X-axis represents Analysis Timepoint for Reporting (3: DAY 0, 4: POST DOSE 1 6 HOUR, 5: POST DOSE 2 6 HOUR, 6: POST DOSE 3 6 HOUR, 7: DAY 4, 8: DAY 7, 9: DAY 10, 10: DAY 15, 99: REPEAT/UNSCHEDULED).
Legend: Artesunate 2.0 mg/kg (blue line with triangles), Artesunate 4.0 mg/kg (orange line with circles), Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg (green line with diamonds), Placebo (red line with squares).
Placebo levels remain low (around 15-20 U/L). Artesunate groups show a transient peak around Day 7 (Artesunate 8.0 mg/kg peaks highest, around 45 U/L), followed by a drop.
